

===== 001 ADEQUACY-001 d7cd1394edd12909fb7d87566fff947829094d6aa06ff725fab1f57891564c75 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
ADEQUACY-001

TITLE:
Precision and adequacy are independent variables.

SOURCE:
User-provided manuscript / conversation synthesis — 2026-08-16.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
A representation can become more detailed, accurate, or benchmark-successful while becoming less adequate to the consequential situation in which it operates.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
RELATIONAL ADEQUACY.

LOCAL MOVE:
Separate ACCURACY OF REPRESENTATION from FIT FOR CONSEQUENCE.

SOURCE TERMS:
precision
adequacy
benchmark
correction
consequence
relational

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Improving measured dimensions can increase institutional confidence while leaving the consequentially relevant missing variable untouched.

QUESTION:
What is the correct relation between statistical accuracy and operational adequacy?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can a representation be Pareto-better on standard metrics while worse for actors bearing correction costs?

MECHANISM:
metric M improves → reliance rises → consequential omitted variable persists → contestation becomes harder → practical adequacy can fall.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<PRECISION ↑> ↛ <ADEQUACY ↑>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Adequate(R | consequence c, actor a, time t, correction channel k), not Adequate(R) simpliciter.

TENSION:
Precision can improve adequacy when the measured variable genuinely matters and correction remains available.

MISSING:
A multidimensional adequacy function that does not merely encode arbitrary stakeholder preferences.

BOUNDARY:
This is not anti-measurement; measurement quality is insufficient evidence of consequential fitness.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[PASSAGE-002]] → loss → adequacy → consequential validity.

TEST:
For one model improvement, measure benchmark accuracy separately from appeal success, error burden, latency, harm severity, and recovery time.

PLATFORM:
[[warranted-passage]]

LINKS:
[[PASSAGE-002]]
[[relational-adequacy]]
[[precision-is-not-adequacy]]

BIBTEX:
NONE


===== 002 AGENCY-001 ca7f2115783a20bd7ab5f590c2c798927f563e97cf64c7805bd8b476b7051237 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
AGENCY-001

TITLE:
A button is not evidence of agency if the causal window has already closed.

SOURCE:
User-provided manuscript / conversation synthesis — 2026-08-16.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Visible choices may appear after upstream classifications, defaults, rankings, permissions, and exclusions have already selected much of the future.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
CAUSAL WINDOW OF AGENCY.

LOCAL MOVE:
Separate FORMAL PARTICIPATION from CAUSAL ACCESS.

SOURCE TERMS:
button
agency
choice
upstream
defaults
permissions
causal window
leverage

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
An interface can maximize apparent choice after the consequential decision has moved elsewhere.

QUESTION:
When is an interface action still causally live?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can agency be measured as expected change in reachable future states rather than number of available controls?

MECHANISM:
upstream system restricts Ω0→Ω1; interface displays actions A1...An; if Reach(Ai) are nearly identical, choice is formally visible but causally weak.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<VISIBLE CHOICE> → [TEST LEVERAGE] → {<CAUSALLY LIVE>, <FORMALLY AVAILABLE BUT DEAD>}

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Leverage(a,s)=divergence between futures under do(a) and baseline.

TENSION:
Symbolic participation can matter through precedent, signaling, coordination, or delayed effects even when immediate leverage is low.

MISSING:
Short-term versus delayed leverage.

BOUNDARY:
Agency should not be reduced entirely to instantaneous outcome control.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[CATEGORY-001]] → upstream binding → interface agency → causal leverage.

TEST:
For each button or appeal mechanism, model downstream state distributions with and without its use. Identify controls whose visibility exceeds causal effect.

PLATFORM:
[[warranted-passage]]

LINKS:
[[CATEGORY-001]]
[[causal-window]]
[[formal-participation]]
[[interface-agency]]

BIBTEX:
NONE


===== 003 BURDEN-001 157794259f19222af2bdbeab37529f6c07f5a7a936140d63bd9b51d3ed1f686c =====

ZETTEL

ID:
BURDEN-001

TITLE:
Representational loss often returns as correction labor borne by bodies.

SOURCE:
User-provided manuscript / conversation synthesis — 2026-08-16.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
What disappears from representation can return as waiting, proving, adapting, translating, exposure, and repeated correction labor.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
REPRESENTATIONAL BURDEN TRANSFER.

LOCAL MOVE:
Treat correction labor not as external friction but as possible downstream transformation of representational omission.

SOURCE TERMS:
body
labor
wait
prove
adapt
correct
translate
burden

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Compression can make a system computationally cheaper by forcing the represented person to perform decompression manually.

QUESTION:
When is user labor the hidden inverse operation of institutional simplification?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can system efficiency be remeasured after adding the labor exported to affected people?

MECHANISM:
system omits x → decision misfits person → person supplies documents/explanations → institution partially reconstructs x → reconsideration.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<INSTITUTIONAL COMPRESSION> → <EXTERNALIZED RECONSTRUCTION LABOR>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
C_total=C_system+C_operator+C_subject_correction. Reducing system cost while increasing subject correction may only relocate cost.

TENSION:
Not every hardship following a decision was caused by the representation; attribution requires counterfactual evidence.

MISSING:
A method separating representationally induced burden from background hardship.

BOUNDARY:
Burden is evidence only when the representational chain materially contributes to generating it.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[PASSAGE-002]] → loss → [[CORRECTION-001]] → correction → burden transfer.

TEST:
Measure service before/after representational simplification: staff time, user documentation, appeals, repeat visits, waiting, successful corrections.

PLATFORM:
[[warranted-passage]]

LINKS:
[[PASSAGE-002]]
[[CORRECTION-001]]
[[administrative-burden]]
[[externalized-labor]]

BIBTEX:
NONE


===== 004 CAREER-001 9157bc8341ae002a607490f9d2061db1395831d7361a1f108fa8d49c0199f556 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
CAREER-001

TITLE:
Geertz’s “shape of its career” makes provenance only half of the path.

SOURCE:
Clifford Geertz — “Art as a Cultural System” — 1976; collected in Local Knowledge — 1983 — Bamileke chief-statue discussion, drawing on Jacques Maquet.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Geertz describes a Bamileke chief’s statue made at accession and subsequently altered in significance as both the physical object and the memory of the chief undergo change. He asks whether the relevant form lies in the statue’s shape or in the shape of its career and answers that both matter.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
ARTIFACT HISTORY HAS AT LEAST TWO DIRECTIONS:

BACKWARD INTO PRODUCTION
and
FORWARD INTO CAREER.

LOCAL MOVE:
The essay’s demand for production paths cannot become another version of origin determinism.

SOURCE TERMS:
statue
chief
memory
weathering
form
career
fate
meaning
force

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Perfect provenance would still fail Geertz’s test.

Knowing every production intervention does not tell us what the object later becomes in collective use.

QUESTION:
How should an account of operative description join production trace to post-production career without reducing one to the other?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can the same artifact acquire new operative roles after leaving the interpretive machinery that produced it?

MECHANISM:
PRODUCTION:

intentions/interventions
→ artifact A_0.

CAREER:

A_0
+
successive audiences/institutions/events
→ uses and interpretations
→ altered status A_t
→ consequences not controlled by maker.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<PROVENANCE TRACE>
→ <ARTIFACT>
→ <CAREER TRACE>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Two paths:

    τ_backward :
    causes → artifact

    τ_forward :
    artifact → consequences.

A complete causal history around artifact A requires both:

    Causes(A)
    and
    Effects(A).

TENSION:
The forward career can feed back into provenance judgments:
later fame, controversy, copying, or institutional classification can change which aspects of production historians consider salient.

MISSING:
A formalism for artifacts whose meanings, classifications, and causal roles change across receiving assemblages.

BOUNDARY:
Production history matters without exhausting meaning.

Reception history matters without retroactively becoming the production mechanism.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[MINIMUM-028]]
→ operative description through receivers
→ Geertz’s natural history of signs
→ Bamileke statue’s career
→ operativity continues after production.

[[CLASS-PATH-001]]
→ backward production path
→ add forward consequence path
→ path becomes career.

TEST:
Choose one artifact with excellent production provenance.

Construct two independent timelines:

A. PRODUCTION DECISIONS
B. POST-RELEASE USES / RECLASSIFICATIONS / CONSEQUENCES.

Identify claims explainable only by B.

PLATFORM:
[[class-is-not-a-path]]

LINKS:
[[MINIMUM-028]]
[[CLASS-PATH-001]]
[[career-of-sign]]
[[provenance-is-not-destiny]]
[[geertz]]

BIBTEX:
@article{Geertz1976Art,
  author  = {Geertz, Clifford},
  title   = {Art as a Cultural System},
  journal = {Modern Language Notes},
  volume  = {91},
  number  = {6},
  pages   = {1473--1499},
  year    = {1976}
}

@book{Geertz1983Local,
  author    = {Geertz, Clifford},
  title     = {Local Knowledge: Further Essays in Interpretive Anthropology},
  publisher = {Basic Books},
  address   = {New York},
  year      = {1983}
}


===== 005 CATEGORY-001 d87f985d38fd0bc76c25c5fdd0dadf29c94eceb0215dff1cf0aa1c2a5ce43d0e =====

ZETTEL

ID:
CATEGORY-001

TITLE:
A category becomes infrastructure when it is compiled through fields, thresholds, permissions, queues, and denials.

SOURCE:
User-provided manuscript / conversation synthesis — 2026-08-16.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
A distinction can move from concept to database field, threshold, permission, interface option, score, queue, denial, and material condition.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
CATEGORY COMPILATION.

LOCAL MOVE:
Replace CATEGORY → CLASSIFICATION with CATEGORY → IMPLEMENTATION CHAIN → CONSEQUENCE.

SOURCE TERMS:
category
database field
threshold
permission
interface option
score
queue
denial

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The most consequential moment may occur long after the category is invented, when one downstream translation makes it binding.

QUESTION:
At which handoff does a representational distinction become materially binding?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can responsibility be attributed to a transformation step rather than only to the category inventor or final decision-maker?

MECHANISM:
conceptual distinction → schema field → encoded value → threshold rule → permission state → interface affordance → administrative action → material consequence.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<CATEGORY> → [COMPILE] → <INFRASTRUCTURAL VARIABLE> → [BIND] → <CONSEQUENCE>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
r0→r1→...→rn→a; each translation may preserve, amplify, suppress, or reinterpret. Binding point b is first layer where category changes reachable actions.

TENSION:
Distributed systems may have multiple binding points and redundant constraints.

MISSING:
A formalism for joint binding and distributed causal contribution.

BOUNDARY:
Distributed causality does not imply responsibility is evenly distributed.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[PASSAGE-001]] → representation enters causal chain → category compilation → infrastructure → binding.

TEST:
Trace one administrative category from policy language to consequence and mark every layer where changing it changes what someone can do.

PLATFORM:
[[warranted-passage]]

LINKS:
[[PASSAGE-001]]
[[category-as-infrastructure]]
[[binding-point]]
[[bureaucratic-ontogenesis]]

BIBTEX:
NONE


===== 006 CIRCULATION-001 141dd961f74cfa747d4363a58c2e2a43c8cba5f2a0cd0bf90f4055d13fb43c66 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
CIRCULATION-001

TITLE:
Circulation is a sequence of transformations, not neutral transport.

SOURCE:
User-provided manuscript / conversation synthesis — 2026-08-16.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Compression, ranking, interface design, recommendation, and institutional interpretation can alter a representation at every transfer. Recognizability does not prove the relation that mattered survived.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
TRANSFORMATIONAL CIRCULATION.

LOCAL MOVE:
Replace “r travels A→B” with r0→τ1(r0)→τ2(...)→rn.

SOURCE TERMS:
circulation
compression
ranking
interface
recommendation
provenance
continuity
translation

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A representation can remain visibly the same while losing precisely the relation for which it was warranted.

QUESTION:
What must survive a transformation for continuity to be claimed?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can provenance record transformation history without already assuming a contested theory of what deserves preservation?

MECHANISM:
source representation → compression → ranking → interface formatting → institutional interpretation → downstream action.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<TRANSPORT> → <SEQUENCE OF LOSSY MORPHISMS>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
For τ:R_i→R_{i+1} and relation q, Preserve(τ,q) must be tested separately; Similar(R_i,R_{i+1}) is insufficient.

TENSION:
Perfect provenance cannot decide which relation ought to survive; preservation criteria are situated.

MISSING:
PRESERVATION AUTHORITY.

BOUNDARY:
Provenance explains what happened; it does not justify the transformation.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[PASSAGE-002]] → recoverability → circulation → provenance → preservation criteria.

TEST:
Trace one datum through five systems and record each input, transformation, output, preserved relation, lost relation, and who defined preservation.

PLATFORM:
[[warranted-passage]]

LINKS:
[[PASSAGE-002]]
[[circulation]]
[[provenance]]
[[preservation-criteria]]

BIBTEX:
NONE


===== 007 CLASS-PATH-001 5006098e37d9752cdc7d44ccc47e2612f503065f5fa4e75f380c9a88c47ea8cf =====

ZETTEL

ID:
CLASS-PATH-001

TITLE:
Burrow-Giles is useful because the Court moves from the camera class to a production path.

SOURCE:
Burrow-Giles Lithographic Co. v. Sarony — 111 U.S. 53 — U.S. Supreme Court — 1884.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
In evaluating Sarony’s photograph of Oscar Wilde, the Court relied on findings describing specific acts: posing Wilde, selecting and arranging costume and draperies, arranging the subject, disposing light and shade, and eliciting a desired expression.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
THE APPARATUS CLASS DID NOT RESOLVE THE CAUSAL QUESTION.

LOCAL MOVE:
The legal analysis moves outward from:

PHOTOGRAPH / CAMERA

toward:

WHAT THE CLAIMED AUTHOR ACTUALLY DID.

SOURCE TERMS:
posing
selecting
arranging
costume
draperies
light
shade
expression
original conception

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The case does not decide whether “photography” as a class is authored.

It examines a particular path of interventions producing a particular photograph.

QUESTION:
Can the methodological move from apparatus category to production trace be separated from the nineteenth-century copyright doctrine surrounding it?

DEEPER QUESTION:
What would the analogous evidentiary trace look like for a probabilistic generative system where requested properties and realized properties can diverge?

MECHANISM:
photographic apparatus
+
posing
+
scene arrangement
+
lighting intervention
+
expression elicitation
→ specific exposure/image.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<MEDIUM CLASS>
→ <CAUSAL PRODUCTION PATH>
→ [ATTRIBUTE SPECIFIC INTERVENTIONS]
→ <AUTHORSHIP FINDING>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Let:

    M = medium/apparatus class

and:

    τ =
    [i₁,i₂,...,i_n]

be the realized intervention trace.

Knowing:

    M

does not determine:

    τ.

TENSION:
Sarony concerns a photographic process in which several expressive variables were directly arranged before capture.

Generative systems may mediate intention less directly.

Therefore the case offers a methodological inheritance, not a technological equivalence.

MISSING:
A principled cross-medium vocabulary for intervention directness and controllability.

BOUNDARY:
The case supports:

CLASSIFICATION OF TOOL ≠ COMPLETE ACCOUNT OF HUMAN CONTRIBUTION.

It does not establish the copyright status of AI-generated material.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[CONTROL-002]]
→ copyright authorship under new image-making machinery
→ Burrow-Giles
→ specific interventions rather than apparatus label
→ class/path distinction.

TEST:
For photography and generative imaging separately, create a causal graph of:

human interventions
machine transformations
environmental variables
random variables
final expressive features.

Compare graphs rather than media labels.

PLATFORM:
[[class-is-not-a-path]]

LINKS:
[[CONTROL-002]]
[[production-path]]
[[photography]]
[[causal-trace]]

BIBTEX:
@misc{BurrowGiles1884,
  title        = {Burrow-Giles Lithographic Co. v. Sarony},
  howpublished = {111 U.S. 53},
  year         = {1884}
}


===== 008 CLASS-PATH-002 8191c8fb50f414483cbe62783a9501155ccf2ab08f178b702baf4c7ac2251be9 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
CLASS-PATH-002

TITLE:
Geertz’s “natural history of signs and symbols” is a direct methodological warrant for replacing class labels with trajectories through use.

SOURCE:
Clifford Geertz — “Art as a Cultural System” — 1976 — conclusion.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Geertz argues that semiotics must leave signs in abstraction and investigate them in the ordinary world in which people use them. He calls for a natural history of signs and symbols and an ethnography of vehicles of meaning.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
“CLASS IS NOT A PATH” has a Geertzian methodological ancestor stronger than the essay currently makes explicit.

LOCAL MOVE:
The argument can move from:

AI LABELS ARE TOO COARSE

to the more general methodological claim:

SIGNS AND TECHNICAL CATEGORIES ACQUIRE EXPLANATORY FORCE THROUGH THEIR CAREERS IN PRACTICE.

SOURCE TERMS:
natural history
signs
symbols
vehicles of meaning
life of a society
use
common world

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Geertz’s objection is not specifically to formalism.

It is to allowing formal description to substitute for tracing how the sign lives.

QUESTION:
Could “AI-generated” itself be studied as a sign whose social career changes the reception of artifacts independently of their production path?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Does the technological label operate twice:
first as a causal descriptor of production,
then as a cultural sign reorganizing judgment?

MECHANISM:
artifact production
→ technical label L attached

then:

audience encounters <artifact,L>
→ label activates expectations/categories
→ perception and judgment shift
→ new career for artifact.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<PRODUCTION CLASSIFICATION>
→ <PUBLIC SIGN>
→ [UPTAKE]
→ <RECEPTION CONSEQUENCE>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Let artifact:

    A

and label:

    L = “AI-generated”.

Reception may satisfy:

    Judge(A,L)
        ≠
    Judge(A,∅)

even though:

    A

is identical.

Thus L is simultaneously:

    metadata about Causes(A)

and:

    causal input into Effects(A).

TENSION:
A label can convey genuinely relevant provenance information.

Showing that it changes judgment does not show that the judgment change is irrational.

MISSING:
Blind/labeled experiments measuring how production-class labels alter judgments while holding artifacts constant.

BOUNDARY:
The critique should not be:

IGNORE THE CLASS.

It should be:

DO NOT SUBSTITUTE THE CLASS FOR EITHER THE PRODUCTION PATH OR THE SIGN’S SUBSEQUENT CAREER.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[CAREER-001]]
→ form includes career
→ Geertz natural history of signs
→ technical label becomes cultural sign
→ label has its own causal history.

[[MINIMUM-028]]
→ receiver and uptake
→ “AI” itself enters receiving system
→ classification becomes operative.

TEST:
Randomly assign identical artifacts to conditions:

AI-GENERATED
HUMAN-MADE
UNLABELED
DETAILED HYBRID PROVENANCE.

Measure changes in:
quality judgment
authorship attribution
intentionality inference
willingness to pay
memory for details.

Then distinguish informationally justified effects from stereotype effects.

PLATFORM:
[[class-is-not-a-path]]

LINKS:
[[CAREER-001]]
[[MINIMUM-028]]
[[label-as-operative-sign]]
[[natural-history-of-signs]]
[[AI-label]]

BIBTEX:
@article{Geertz1976Art,
  author  = {Geertz, Clifford},
  title   = {Art as a Cultural System},
  journal = {Modern Language Notes},
  volume  = {91},
  number  = {6},
  pages   = {1473--1499},
  year    = {1976}
}


===== 009 CONSTITUTION-001 768d8c365351a0b3f0676a8c9bbdf6e67b115e6f87163098d266e681d3701813 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
CONSTITUTION-001

TITLE:
Representation can help make reality without exhausting the reality it helps make.

SOURCE:
User-provided manuscript / conversation synthesis — 2026-08-16.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Categories, scores, forecasts, recommendations, and models can alter behavior and future conditions, yet their causal productivity does not make the world nothing but representation.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
CONSTITUTION WITHOUT TOTAL CONSTRUCTION.

LOCAL MOVE:
Escape the binary NAIVE REALISM versus TOTAL CONSTRUCTIVISM.

SOURCE TERMS:
constitutive
category
score
forecast
recommendation
make future
ontologically exhaustive

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A strong causal claim about representation can coexist with a strong realism about what exceeds it.

QUESTION:
What exactly does a representation produce when it “makes” a population, market, behavior, or future?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can constitutive effects be decomposed into category availability, institutional response, self-modification, resource allocation, and material feedback?

MECHANISM:
R → alters incentives/opportunities/status → actors/institutions respond → W changes → later representation observes changed W.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<REPRESENTATION OF WORLD> → [INTERVENTION] → <PARTLY REPRESENTATION-SHAPED WORLD> without W=R

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
∂W_{t+1}/∂R_t ≠ 0 does not imply W_t=R_t.

TENSION:
Some constructionist theories question whether W can be cleanly specified independently of representational practice.

MISSING:
How to describe extra-representational constraints without reintroducing naive objectivism.

BOUNDARY:
Causal productivity is weaker than ontological exhaustiveness.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[SCALE-001]] → self-producing magnitudes → performativity → constitution without total construction.

TEST:
For one classification list conditions it helps create, merely records, cannot contain, and conditions causally independent of deployment.

PLATFORM:
[[warranted-passage]]

LINKS:
[[SCALE-001]]
[[constitution-without-total-construction]]
[[performative-model]]
[[reality-making]]

BIBTEX:
NONE


===== 010 CONTROL-001 9e9b483c89f361e4bbbd0e94f0566845fc668307cbf9e263ef759f067440630d =====

ZETTEL

ID:
CONTROL-001

TITLE:
“Call the shot” converts skill from retrospective attribution into a prospective falsifiable claim.

SOURCE:
Roger Sharpe’s 1976 New York City pinball testimony, as reconstructed in later interviews and University of Wisconsin alumni history.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Sharpe’s demonstration of pinball skill involved announcing intended shots and then attempting them before the City Council. Later accounts emphasize his practice of calling trajectories in advance and his understanding of the game’s geometry and sequences.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
PROSPECTIVE SPECIFICATION is stronger evidence of control than retrospective selection.

LOCAL MOVE:
The essay’s “call the shot” test can be formalized without making it a complete theory of authorship.

SOURCE TERMS:
call the shot
skill
geometry
sequence
control
trajectory

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
After an outcome occurs, a maker can always redescribe an attractive accident as intended.

A prospective prediction closes that loophole partially by exposing intention before the system resolves uncertainty.

QUESTION:
What exactly does successful shot-calling measure:
prediction,
control,
skill,
causal intervention,
or calibration?

DEEPER QUESTION:
How many successful counterfactual interventions are required before we can infer robust control rather than luck?

MECHANISM:
maker announces target property y*
→ acts through interface a
→ stochastic/physical system evolves
→ outcome y
→ compare y to y*
→ repeat under nearby conditions.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<RETROSPECTIVE CLAIM OF INTENTION>
→ [PRECOMMIT TARGET]
→ <FALSIFIABLE CONTROL CLAIM>
→ [SYSTEM RESPONSE]
→ <SUCCESS / FAILURE>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE.

The pinball episode is a practical demonstration, not a statistical test of causal control.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

A minimal control experiment:

    declare target τ before action

    do(a)

    observe Y

    success = [Y ∈ τ].

Stronger evidence requires repeated interventions:

    P(Y ∈ τ | do(a_targeted))
        >
    P(Y ∈ τ | baseline).

TENSION:
One spectacular successful called shot can still occur by chance.

Sharpe’s historical demonstration was rhetorically persuasive without being a controlled experiment.

MISSING:
A statistical/control-theoretic version of “call the shot” suitable for generative interfaces.

BOUNDARY:
Prospective success provides evidence of control.

It does not by itself establish artistic authorship, creativity, or legal protectability.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[UPTAKE-003]]
→ counterfactual control
→ Roger Sharpe called shots
→ prospective intervention
→ control as falsifiable difference-making.

TEST:
For a generative system, define 20 nearby transformations in advance:

preserve A
change B to specified target B*
hold C within tolerance.

Record success before selection or curation.

Compare against blind sampling and post hoc cherry-picking.

PLATFORM:
[[class-is-not-a-path]]

LINKS:
[[UPTAKE-003]]
[[counterfactual-control]]
[[pinball]]
[[prospective-test]]

BIBTEX:
@misc{Sharpe1976Pinball,
  author = {Sharpe, Roger},
  title  = {Testimony concerning New York City's pinball ban},
  year   = {1976},
  note   = {Historical event; primary transcript still to be located}
}


===== 011 CONTROL-002 2aa4c2019eff6cbc984d477e570185a7b4c5c451d6f5f25ce2c1f7916eb347de =====

ZETTEL

ID:
CONTROL-002

TITLE:
The Copyright Office recorded 624 iterations but treated the number as irrelevant to whether Allen controlled the particular expressive result.

SOURCE:
U.S. Copyright Office Review Board — Decision Affirming Refusal to Register Théâtre D’opéra Spatial — September 5, 2023 — pp. 2, 6.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
The administrative record states that Jason Allen reported at least 624 revisions and text prompts before arriving at the initial Midjourney image. The Board nevertheless focused on whether those prompts determined specific expressive elements of the generated result, concluding that the outcome depended on how Midjourney processed the prompts.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
ITERATION COUNT and EXPRESSIVE CONTROL are different variables.

LOCAL MOVE:
The essay’s “odometer versus steering” distinction is unusually well aligned with the structure of the official record.

SOURCE TERMS:
624
revisions
text prompts
creative input
influence
specific expressive result
human authorship

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The Board had exactly the evidence that popular discussions often demand:

A LARGE NUMBER OF PROMPTS.

It did not treat that quantity as dispositive evidence of authorship over the generated image.

QUESTION:
What evidence WOULD distinguish repeated search from repeatable control over expressive elements?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can a legal inquiry into human authorship be operationalized using counterfactual control tests without collapsing legal authorship into engineering controllability?

MECHANISM:
Allen:
prompt revision
→ output
→ inspect
→ revise
→ output
→ repeat ≥624 times.

Board’s question:
did human input determine traditional expressive elements,
or did system processing supply them?

FORMAL SHIFT:
<WORKFLOW LENGTH>
≠
<CONTROL OVER PARTICULAR EXPRESSIVE FEATURES>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE.

The source applies copyright doctrine case-by-case, not a quantitative control metric.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Iteration length:

    L = number of generation/revision cycles.

Control over feature f:

    C_f =
    ability to intentionally set f
    while holding specified neighboring features invariant.

No implication follows automatically:

    large L
    → large C_f.

TENSION:
Repeated iteration can be the process through which a practitioner discovers and exercises genuine control.

The number is therefore not meaningless; it is simply non-diagnostic by itself.

MISSING:
Allen’s actual sequence of prompts and outputs.

Without it, the 624-step path cannot be reconstructed at feature level.

BOUNDARY:
The Office’s decision is evidence about U.S. copyright authorship doctrine in this specific record.

It is not a general scientific verdict that prompting cannot constitute control.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[CONTROL-001]]
→ prospective control test
→ Théâtre D’opéra Spatial
→ 624 iterations
→ iteration quantity fails to reveal steering.

[[UPTAKE-003]]
→ counterfactual control
→ legal record supplies unusually explicit conflict between effort and expressive control.

TEST:
Obtain or reconstruct a generation history.

For each revision classify:

TARGET DECLARED BEFORE OUTPUT
FEATURE CHANGED AS REQUESTED
FEATURE PRESERVED AS REQUESTED
UNREQUESTED ATTRACTIVE CHANGE
POST-HOC SELECTION.

Compute control evidence independently of total prompt count.

PLATFORM:
[[class-is-not-a-path]]

LINKS:
[[CONTROL-001]]
[[UPTAKE-003]]
[[prompt-count]]
[[authorship]]
[[steering-vs-search]]

===== 012 CORRECTION-001 b96fe13ad33b012818e0f86fcbe723bafb26a15e965f9e4090f59aa68586f815 =====

ID:
CORRECTION-001

TITLE:
Correction is only real when the contested difference survives translation into a still-open intervention point.

SOURCE:
User-provided manuscript / conversation synthesis — 2026-08-16.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Systems can offer speech, participation, appeal, or visibility while remaining operationally closed if dissent is translated into categories that erase the contested difference or arrives after consequence is fixed.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
CAUSALLY EFFECTIVE CORRECTION.

LOCAL MOVE:

SOURCE TERMS:

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A correction mechanism can be perfectly available and perfectly incapable of carrying the correction.

QUESTION:
What distinguishes an appeal channel from an actual correction channel?

DEEPER QUESTION:

MECHANISM:

FORMAL SHIFT:

NONE

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

TENSION:

===== 013 FUTAMURA-001 16400960b490c7ae9b92b4fc2ea82778caabb8818f136f491de8c696e20cfa87 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
FUTAMURA-001

TITLE:
Compilation can be obtained by freezing the source program inside its interpreter.

SOURCE:
Neil D. Jones, Carsten K. Gomard, and Peter Sestoft — Partial Evaluation and Automatic Program Generation — 1993 — §1.5.1 and §4.3.2–4.3.3; following Yoshihiko Futamura’s 1971 result.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Specializing an interpreter with respect to one fixed source program produces a target program implementing that source program. The authors characterize the result as compilation by partial evaluation and describe the target as a specialized interpreter.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
COMPILER and INTERPRETER cease to be fundamentally different mechanisms.

Compilation emerges by staging interpretation.

LOCAL MOVE:
The parent asked what distinguishes program from input.

Futamura makes the source program simultaneously:

INPUT TO THE INTERPRETER

and:

STATIC MATERIAL FROM WHICH TARGET CODE IS GENERATED.

SOURCE TERMS:
interpreter
source program
target program
specialization
first Futamura projection
compilation

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The target program contains pieces of the interpreter specialized by information extracted from the source program.

Neither SOURCE PROGRAM nor INTERPRETER alone survives unchanged as the executable artifact.

QUESTION:
Is compiled code best understood as a program translation or as an interpreter partially evaluated into its input?

DEEPER QUESTION:
If interpreter machinery can disappear into residual code, where should the boundary between semantics and program representation be located?

MECHANISM:
interpreter int
+
source program source
→ specializer mix
→ target

such that:

target(input)

produces the same result as:

int(source,input).

FORMAL SHIFT:
<INTERPRETER + PROGRAM-AS-INPUT>
→ [SPECIALIZATION]
→ <TARGET PROGRAM>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
The source gives the first Futamura projection:

    target = [[mix]] [int, source]

and establishes its behavior through the interpreter and specialization equations.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Before specialization:

    I(source,x)

After specialization:

    I_source(x)

where:

    I_source = Specialize(I,source).

Thus compiled programhood can be seen as:

    INTERPRETER RESIDUALIZED WITH RESPECT TO SOURCE.

TENSION:
A hand-written compiler need not literally be generated by specialization, so this is an existence/construction result rather than the only ontology of compilation.

MISSING:
Which semantic distinctions between:
INTERPRETER,
COMPILER,
and
TARGET PROGRAM

survive all semantics-preserving staging transformations?

BOUNDARY:
Interpreter and compiler are operational roles that can be transformed into one another under suitable program-specialization machinery.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[UPTAKE-003]]
→ program/input distinction
→ Futamura first projection
→ source as static interpreter input
→ target as specialized interpreter
→ role boundaries become staging boundaries.

TEST:
Implement a tiny interpreter and specialize it to one source program.

Compare the generated target with:
the source syntax,
the interpreter syntax,
and a hand-written compiled target.

Measure where each piece of operational machinery came from.

PLATFORM:
[[description-becomes-operation]]

LINKS:
[[UPTAKE-003]]
[[first-futamura-projection]]
[[interpreter-vs-compiler]]
[[program-role]]

  publisher = {Prentice Hall},
  year      = {1993}
}

===== 014 FUTAMURA-002 570edb64a4ff0ed7a76f848e409586c7b35c563d54b53a96b52497081a45a5e1 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
FUTAMURA-002

TITLE:
Self-application turns the specializer into a compiler and then into a compiler generator.

SOURCE:
Neil D. Jones, Carsten K. Gomard, and Peter Sestoft — Partial Evaluation and Automatic Program Generation — 1993 — §§1.5.2–1.5.3, §4.3.2.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
When a self-applicable partial evaluator is specialized with respect to an interpreter, the residual program is a compiler for the interpreter’s source language. When the partial evaluator is specialized with respect to itself, the residual artifact is a compiler generator.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
PROGRAMMING ROLES can emerge from self-application rather than from distinct primitive machinery.

LOCAL MOVE:
The parent’s rules-of-rule-change problem acquires a concrete computational mechanism.

The machine that transforms programs can itself become its own input.

SOURCE TERMS:
self-application
partial evaluator
compiler
compiler generator
Futamura projection
mix

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
One program text, `mix`, can participate as:

EXECUTING SPECIALIZER
INPUT PROGRAM
COMPILER CONSTRUCTOR
and
MATERIAL FOR COMPILER-GENERATOR CONSTRUCTION.

QUESTION:
Are interpreter, compiler, and compiler-generator different kinds of object or different fixed arguments of a sufficiently general higher-order operator?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Does self-application imply that computational role is fundamentally relational rather than syntactic?

MECHANISM:
First:

    target = mix(int, source)

Second:

    compiler = mix(mix, int)

Third:

    cogen = mix(mix, mix).

FORMAL SHIFT:
<SPECIALIZER>
→ [SELF-APPLICATION + FIXED INTERPRETER]
→ <COMPILER>
→ [SELF-APPLICATION AGAIN]
→ <COMPILER GENERATOR>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Jones et al. give the equations:

    target   = [[mix]] [int, source]
    compiler = [[mix]] [mix, int]
    cogen    = [[mix]] [mix, mix]

as the Futamura projections.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Role may be modeled as:

    Role(object, argument-position, stage)

rather than:

    TypeOfThing(object).

The same underlying program can move among meta-level roles depending on where it is supplied.

TENSION:
Type constraints and language boundaries still restrict which self-applications are valid.

Not every specializer is self-applicable.

MISSING:
What structural properties make self-application possible without semantic collapse or nontermination?

BOUNDARY:
Meta-level status can be generated operationally through staging rather than residing intrinsically in an artifact.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[METASTABLE-001]]
→ rules of rule-change
→ self-applicable partial evaluator
→ compiler and compiler-generator generation
→ meta-role as executable staging relation.

[[REFLECT-001]]
→ language implementation as program
→ specializer applied to itself
→ programmable implementation generation.

TEST:
For each occurrence of `mix` in the three Futamura equations label it:

EXECUTED PROGRAM
STATIC INPUT
GENERATED OUTPUT.

If the same artifact occupies all three positions, reject any ontology assigning those roles intrinsically.

PLATFORM:
[[description-becomes-operation]]

LINKS:
[[METASTABLE-001]]
[[REFLECT-001]]
[[self-application]]
[[futamura-projections]]
[[meta-role]]

===== 015 MARKOV-ONEGIN-001 57fd63ff4f3763b6e13f913c77aa3cdd81f3b6f99d5e5d5829d74f882deba3d7 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
MARKOV-ONEGIN-001

TITLE:
Markov did not build a model of Eugene Onegin’s poetry; he destroyed almost all of the poem to isolate dependence.

SOURCE:
A. A. Markov — “An Example of Statistical Investigation of the Text of Eugene Onegin Illustrating Coupling of ‘Tests’ in Chains” — 1913 — pp. 153–162.
Historical reconstruction: Brian Hayes — “First Links in the Markov Chain” — 2013.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Markov takes 20,000 letters from Eugene Onegin and reduces them to a binary sequence based on whether each letter is a vowel or consonant. He studies statistical dependence between successive classifications rather than meter, syntax, semantics, narrative, character, or poetic intention.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
MARKOV’S REDUCTION IS DELIBERATELY ANTI-POETIC.

Its success depends on discarding almost everything the work is culturally valued for.

LOCAL MOVE:
The essay’s “fossil” metaphor can be made precise as a projection:

POEM
→ BINARY SYMBOL SERIES.

SOURCE TERMS:
statistical investigation
connected trials
chain
vowel
consonant
20,000 letters

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Markov’s experiment does not demonstrate:

POETRY IS A MARKOV CHAIN.

It demonstrates:

ONE STATISTICAL PROPERTY OF ONE PROJECTION OF A POEM EXHIBITS DEPENDENCE.

QUESTION:
How much of the later genealogy from Markov to “language models” depends on forgetting this projection step?

DEEPER QUESTION:
When does a deliberately lossy analytic representation become mistaken for an ontology of the object represented?

MECHANISM:
Eugene Onegin
→ delete most linguistic/artistic distinctions
→ classify each surviving letter V/C
→ count adjacent relationships
→ estimate dependence.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<RICH POETIC OBJECT>
→ [COARSE-GRAIN]
→ <BINARY SEQUENCE>
→ [STATISTICAL ANALYSIS]
→ <DEPENDENCE RELATION>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
A sequence of 20,000 connected trials classified into two categories, vowel and consonant, with frequencies of neighboring category combinations used to study dependence.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Let:

    X = full textual/artistic object

and projection:

    π : X → {V,C}^20000.

Markov studies:

    P(π_{t+1} | π_t)

not:

    P(next poetic act | cultural history).

TENSION:
The violence of the reduction is exactly what gives the experiment mathematical clarity.

Loss is methodological power here, not methodological error.

MISSING:
A genealogy of when Markov’s analytic model was later reinterpreted as a generative theory of linguistic sequence.

BOUNDARY:
The experiment is evidence that selective reduction can reveal a genuine relation without exhausting its source object.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[MARKOV-POET-001]]
→ Geertz uses Markov as generative analogy
→ Markov 1913 source
→ original experiment is analytic reduction
→ distinguish generative process from statistical projection.

TEST:
List every feature of Eugene Onegin eliminated by the V/C projection.

Then identify exactly which conclusions remain invariant under radically different texts sharing the same adjacent V/C statistics.

PLATFORM:
[[the-markov-poet]]

LINKS:
[[MARKOV-POET-001]]
[[reduction]]
[[projection]]
[[eugene-onegin]]
[[class-is-not-path]]

  journal = {Proceedings of the Academy of Sciences of St. Petersburg},

===== 016 MARKOV-ONEGIN-002 6196fcf49536c1df14031aee2d94285bb909e7d5785a59c8c86d460468d6883b =====

ZETTEL

ID:
MARKOV-ONEGIN-002

TITLE:
Eugene Onegin was evidence in Markov’s fight about independence, not the object his mathematics was designed to explain.

SOURCE:
A. A. Markov — work on chains of dependent trials, 1906–1913.
Brian Hayes — “First Links in the Markov Chain” — 2013 — historical reconstruction of Markov’s dispute with Pavel Nekrasov.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Nekrasov had connected the law of large numbers to statistical independence and extended that connection into arguments about voluntary action and free will. Markov developed limit results for dependent sequences, undermining the necessity of independence. His later Onegin calculation provided a concrete sequence in which adjacent events were dependent.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
PUSHKIN IS AN EXPERIMENTAL WEAPON IN A PROBABILITY DISPUTE.

LOCAL MOVE:
The essay’s line:

“Markov’s stripped poem is aimed at no one. Markov is.”

can be hardened historically.

The target of the reduction is an argument about independence.

SOURCE TERMS:
independence
dependent trials
law of large numbers
Nekrasov
free will
chains

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The most famous early mathematical encounter between probability and literature was not primarily an attempt to understand language.

Literature supplied a convenient observable chain.

QUESTION:
Why did Markov choose literary text as his empirical demonstration rather than another dependent sequence?

DEEPER QUESTION:
How often do later “models of culture” originate as demonstrations of mathematical machinery whose original explanatory target lay elsewhere?

MECHANISM:
Nekrasov:
LLN
→ asserted dependence on independence
→ philosophical extension toward voluntary acts.

Markov:
construct theory for dependent trials
→ show limit behavior survives dependence
→ seek empirical chain
→ reduce Pushkin to V/C sequence.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<PHILOSOPHICAL/MATHEMATICAL CLAIM ABOUT INDEPENDENCE>
→ [COUNTEREXAMPLE THEORY]
→ <DEPENDENT CHAINS>
→ [LITERARY DEMONSTRATION]
→ <ONEGIN STATISTICS>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Markov’s mathematical work extends probability results to dependent chains.

The historical free-will genealogy is supplied by later scholarship and should not be treated as a quotation from the 1913 Onegin paper.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
NONE

TENSION:
The independence/free-will dispute is well documented in historical reconstructions, but its exact causal role in Markov’s choice of Eugene Onegin requires careful archival support.

MISSING:
Markov’s own correspondence or prefatory remarks explicitly connecting the Onegin experiment to Nekrasov.

BOUNDARY:
The intellectual target can be reconstructed more confidently than Markov’s private motive for choosing Pushkin.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[MARKOV-ONEGIN-001]]
→ why this reduction?
→ Markov/Nekrasov controversy
→ dependent trials as counterexample
→ Pushkin as demonstration rather than linguistic object.

TEST:
Find Markov’s own statements between 1906 and 1913 naming:
Nekrasov,
independence,
or the reason for applying chain analysis to literature.

Separate documented motive from retrospective historical synthesis.

PLATFORM:
[[the-markov-poet]]

LINKS:

[[nekrasov]]
[[independence]]
[[mathematical-target]]

===== 017 MARKOV-POET-001 fb4b6ce4c547e72eefcc87abd6cc7bc0c31eca5637e3532ce37adc43d7eef832 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
MARKOV-POET-001

TITLE:
Geertz’s “artistic Markov process” is immediately subordinated to composition-in-performance.

SOURCE:
Clifford Geertz — “Art as a Cultural System” — 1976 — Moroccan oral-poetry discussion.
Albert B. Lord — The Singer of Tales — 1960 — composition-in-performance lineage explicitly invoked by Geertz.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Geertz says the Moroccan poet builds the poem incrementally from a bounded repertoire of thematic, figurative, and formal formulae, comparing this local assembly process to an “artistic Markov process.” But he immediately embeds that molecular construction inside singing, percussion, dance, genre demands, approval, and censure, insisting that the poem cannot be separated from the performance and is continually remade as an act.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The Markov analogy applies to one layer of production:

FORMULA SELECTION / SEQUENTIAL ASSEMBLY.

It does not describe the whole ontology of the poem.

LOCAL MOVE:
The essay’s strongest Geertz passage contains its own warning against reducing the cultural act to its generative mechanism.

SOURCE TERMS:
artistic Markov process
formulae
molecularly
singing
audience
approval
censure
event
act

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Geertz simultaneously says:

THE POEM IS MARKOV-LIKE

and:

THE POEM CANNOT BE ABSTRACTED FROM THE PERFORMANCE.

The apparent contradiction disappears if the Markov description is only one projection of a larger event.

QUESTION:
What exactly is the object to which Geertz applies the Markov analogy:
the sequence of verses,
formula selection,
poetic competence,
or the entire performance?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can one process possess a perfectly useful low-dimensional generative description while that description remains radically insufficient as an account of the act?

MECHANISM:
inherited formula stock
+
meter/rhyme constraints
→ local verse construction

embedded within:

poet
+
audience
+
target
+
music
+
dance
+
approval/censure
→ unfolding performance event.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<WHOLE CULTURAL EVENT>
→ [PROJECT ONTO SEQUENTIAL FORMULA SELECTION]
→ <MARKOV-LIKE DESCRIPTION>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE.

Geertz offers a comparison, not a transition matrix, state definition, Markov property, or estimated stochastic model.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Let:

    P = full performance process

and:

    π(P) = sequence of formula selections.

A Markov approximation might describe:

    π(P)

without implying:

    P = π(P).

TENSION:
The metaphor may still contain more technical content than casual ornament because Geertz stresses bounded formula stock and piecewise sequential construction.

But nothing in the passage establishes the Markov property formally.

MISSING:
Evidence concerning what Geertz knew about Markov models and whether “Markov process” was intended technically or colloquially.

BOUNDARY:
The passage supports:

FORMULAIC GENERATION IS PART OF THE POEM’S MAKING.

It does not support:

THE POEM IS EXHAUSTIVELY EXPLAINED BY A MARKOV PROCESS.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[MINIMUM-025]]
→ underdetermined cultural production
→ Geertz’s Moroccan poet
→ Markov-like molecular construction
→ performance exceeds projected generative description.

[[MINIMUM-028]]
→ operative description
→ same event admits multiple descriptive resolutions
→ mechanism is not whole causal history.

TEST:
Take Geertz’s performance description and assign every described element to one of:

SEQUENTIAL GENERATION
FORMAL CONSTRAINT
FEEDBACK
SOCIAL AUTHORITY
ECONOMIC INCENTIVE
TARGET SELECTION
BODILY PERFORMANCE
AUDIENCE RESPONSE.

Then ask which columns survive if the event is represented only as a first-order Markov chain over formulae.

[[the-markov-poet]]

[[MINIMUM-028]]
[[geertz]]
[[oral-formulaic-composition]]
[[projection-is-not-event]]

===== 018 MARKOV-POET-002 17703af4ec957fad9e3d431c5dccdaaf15eb40bef2b8f9b11031aba9c75f5242 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
MARKOV-POET-002

TITLE:
The audience is not context around the Moroccan poem; it is an input channel into its production.

SOURCE:
Clifford Geertz — “Art as a Cultural System” — 1976 — Moroccan poetry passage.
Albert B. Lord — The Singer of Tales — 1960.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Geertz includes audience approval and whistles of censure among the elements making up the integral poetic event. Lord’s oral-formulaic account likewise treats composition and performance as aspects of one act rather than a finished text followed by delivery.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
FEEDBACK enters the generative mechanism before the artifact is complete.

LOCAL MOVE:
The essay’s distinction between Markov’s fossilized Pushkin and Geertz’s living poet becomes technically sharper:

OPEN-LOOP SEQUENCE ANALYSIS
versus
CLOSED-LOOP GENERATION UNDER FEEDBACK.

SOURCE TERMS:
performance
composition
audience
approval
censure
formula
act

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
If the audience’s reaction can alter what the poet does next, then treating the audience as external CONTEXT understates its causal role.

It belongs inside the state-transition architecture.

QUESTION:
Can the Moroccan performance be represented more faithfully as a controlled or feedback-coupled stochastic process than as an ordinary Markov chain?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Does the crucial distinction from contemporary generative systems concern stochastic generation at all, or access to consequential feedback while generation is still unfolding?

MECHANISM:
performance state s_t
+
poet action a_t
→ verse/performance event
→ audience response o_t
→ poet perceives o_t
→ altered choice a_{t+1}
→ next performance state.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<SEQUENCE GENERATOR>
→ <GENERATOR + OBSERVER RESPONSE>
→ [FEEDBACK]
→ <ADAPTIVE TRAJECTORY>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE.

The source documents feedback-like interaction but does not formulate a controlled stochastic process.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

A candidate model is:

    s_t
    → poet chooses a_t
    → environment/audience produces o_t
    → s_{t+1} ~ K(. | s_t,a_t,o_t)

with policy:

    a_t ~ π(. | history_t).

If the poet’s policy depends on remembered history beyond s_t, the chosen state representation must be enlarged before the process is Markov.

TENSION:
Any sufficiently rich state can often be made Markov by encoding enough history.

That mathematical maneuver risks deleting the very question of which remembered distinctions matter culturally.

MISSING:
Ethnographic evidence of specific cases where an audience response changed the next verse, target, or rhetorical strategy.

BOUNDARY:
Feedback is documented as constitutive of the performance.

A technical MDP/control interpretation remains OUR FORMALIZATION.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[MARKOV-POET-001]]
→ audience inside integral whole
→ controlled Markov processes
→ feedback becomes state input
→ search fieldnotes for actual adaptive episodes.

[[UPTAKE-002]]
→ rule versus strategy
→ poet’s formula stock versus situated selection policy.

TEST:
Locate one transcript or fieldnote containing:

VERSE_t
AUDIENCE RESPONSE_t
VERSE_{t+1}.

Demonstrate whether the second verse contains a change plausibly attributable to the intervening feedback.

PLATFORM:
[[the-markov-poet]]

LINKS:
[[MARKOV-POET-001]]
[[UPTAKE-002]]
[[feedback]]
[[composition-in-performance]]
[[controlled-process]]

  volume  = {91},

}

===== 019 MARKOV-REVIVAL-001 b43a1632644462908d83c7591a56044e90c3eaf799e6893ab0d27a14da2f6345 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
MARKOV-REVIVAL-001

TITLE:
Geertz’s 1976 Markov metaphor and the HMM turn in speech recognition are a real chronological collision with no demonstrated genealogy between them.

SOURCE:
Clifford Geertz — “Art as a Cultural System” — 1976.
James K. Baker — “Stochastic Modeling as a Means of Automatic Speech Recognition” — Carnegie Mellon University doctoral work — 1975.
Carnegie Mellon historical accounts of speech recognition.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Geertz publishes the phrase “artistic Markov process” in 1976. At Carnegie Mellon, James Baker’s mid-1970s work applies hidden Markov modeling to speech recognition, part of an emerging statistical approach that later became highly consequential in automatic speech recognition.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
THE COINCIDENCE IS STRONGER THAN THE GENEALOGY.

LOCAL MOVE:
The essay’s timing joke survives if stated as parallel chronology rather than intellectual transmission.

SOURCE TERMS:
Markov process
hidden Markov model
speech recognition
stochastic modeling
1975
1976

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
At almost the same historical moment:

one field could treat Markov as an old reductive model of language,

another could be discovering that Markovian latent-state machinery was extraordinarily productive for recognizing speech,

and Geertz could deploy “Markov” casually inside anthropology.

QUESTION:
Was Geertz aware of contemporary statistical speech-recognition research?

DEEPER QUESTION:
How can one mathematical form be simultaneously declining as a THEORY OF LANGUAGE and ascending as an ENGINEERING MODEL OF SIGNAL-TO-LANGUAGE INFERENCE?

MECHANISM:
LINGUISTICS:
finite-state Markov grammar
→ judged inadequate for syntactic description.

SPEECH ENGINEERING:
acoustic observations
→ hidden-state stochastic model
→ recognition.

ANTHROPOLOGY:
formulaic oral composition
→ “artistic Markov process” analogy.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<ONE MATHEMATICAL FAMILY>
→ {
    <REJECTED LINGUISTIC GRAMMAR>,
    <SUCCESSFUL SPEECH ENGINEERING TOOL>,
    <ANTHROPOLOGICAL METAPHOR>
  }

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Baker’s work concerns hidden-state stochastic models for speech recognition.

Geertz supplies no HMM machinery.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
NONE

TENSION:
Chronological simultaneity is intellectually suggestive but proves no influence.

“Markov looked old” also requires disciplinary reception evidence rather than inference from Chomsky alone.

MISSING:
Citation/reception studies of Markov models in linguistics between 1957 and 1976.

Any evidence that Geertz encountered Baker, speech-recognition research, or information-processing discussions of HMMs.

BOUNDARY:
The juxtaposition is historically defensible.

The claim that Geertz’s metaphor was “safe because Markov had lost” remains [OUR INFERENCE] until reception evidence is found.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[MARKOV-POET-001]]
→ Geertz 1976
→ Baker 1975
→ contemporaneous uses of Markov machinery
→ distinguish chronology from genealogy.

TEST:
Search 1957–1976 linguistics and anthropology journals for:

“Markov process”
“Markov chain”
“finite-state”
“hidden Markov”

Plot citation frequency and disciplinary context.

Then inspect Geertz’s papers/notes for any direct Markov-related source.

PLATFORM:
[[the-markov-poet]]

LINKS:
[[MARKOV-POET-001]]
[[chronology-not-causation]]
[[speech-recognition]]
[[HMM]]

===== 020 METASTABLE-001 8b4f39c934266cbc70ff540e4444a5dc6db91d624369904aef9268fc887f5a60 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
METASTABLE-001

TITLE:
Reflection shifts the cultural/computational boundary from “can the rules change?” to “what constrains legitimate rule-change?”

SOURCE:
COLLISION — Gregor Kiczales, Jim des Rivières, and Daniel G. Bobrow — The Art of the Metaobject Protocol — 1991; Francisco Durán et al. — “Programming and Symbolic Computation in Maude” — 2019.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Metaobject protocols deliberately expose a region of language behavior that users may modify, while reflective rewriting logic can represent rule theories as manipulable objects. In both cases, computational semantics can become a target of computation rather than remaining an immutable background.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
META-MUTABILITY survives the destructive test.

But it does not yet erase all distinction.

The new unresolved variable is the RULES OF RULE CHANGE.

LOCAL MOVE:
The parent’s candidate boundary:

SOCIAL:
rules may change

COMPUTATIONAL:
rules fixed

is killed.

A stronger candidate becomes:

HOW IS THE SPACE OF LEGITIMATE META-CHANGE DELIMITED?

SOURCE TERMS:
reflection
metaobject protocol
region of behavior
universal theory
metalevel
language behavior
rule

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Kiczales explicitly conceptualizes not one fixed language but a REGION of possible languages reachable through an exposed protocol.

This resembles institutional variation more than the classical fixed-semantics picture did.

QUESTION:
Is computational reflection distinguished from social reflexivity because its meta-changes remain typed and delimited by a pre-existing meta-language?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can a system generate a genuinely new semantic distinction that was not representable in the ontology of its meta-level before the change?

MECHANISM:
base semantics M₀
→ expose metaobjects / represented rules
→ legal metalevel operations
→ M₁
→ altered base behavior.

But:

legal meta-operations
are themselves constrained by
meta-language / universal interpreter.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<FIXED SEMANTICS>
→ <MUTABLE SEMANTICS>
→ [META-RULES]
→ <CONSTRAINED SPACE OF SEMANTIC VARIATION>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Kiczales et al. describe a protocol-defined region of language behaviors around a default.

Maude represents rewrite theories inside a universal reflective theory.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Let:

    M_t = current semantics
    Ω   = space of representable semantic modifications.

Then:

    MetaStep : M_t × m ∈ Ω → M_{t+1}

The new question is whether Ω itself is fixed.

A stronger reflexive case would permit:

    Ω_t → Ω_{t+1}.

TENSION:
Reflective towers and self-representation make it difficult to identify a simple final meta-level.

Conversely, social institutions also contain explicit constitutions, amendment procedures, professional jurisdictions, and vocabularies that constrain what counts as legitimate change.

MISSING:
A comparison between:

metaobject protocols
reflective rewrite theories
constitutional amendment
scientific paradigm/category revision
institutional reclassification.

BOUNDARY:
“Rules can change” is no longer a viable absolute separator.

The live boundary candidate is representability and governance of meta-change.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[MINIMUM-026]]
→ self-modifying semantic systems
→ metaobject protocols
→ reflective rewriting logic
→ rules of rule-change.

[[MINIMUM-025]]
→ ontology instability
→ possibility that the transition ontology itself changes
→ meta-ontology as new target.

TEST:
Construct three systems:

A. ordinary fixed interpreter
B. reflective interpreter that can alter predefined semantic hooks
C. system that can introduce a new category of semantic hook not previously representable.

For each identify:

    what may change
    what cannot change
    who authorizes change
    how new distinctions become representable.

PLATFORM:
[[description-becomes-operation]]

LINKS:
[[MINIMUM-025]]
[[MINIMUM-026]]
[[meta-mutability]]
[[rules-of-rule-change]]
[[ontology-change]]
[[reflection]]

===== 021 MINIMUM-024 38d3eb511dcb5cc5fa0e5ebdc00a4f16264bdffef08c46cfe87256b07f09367f =====

ZETTEL

ID:
MINIMUM-024

TITLE:
LLMs did not create the first machine system in which ordinary English commands changed a computational world.

SOURCE:
Terry Winograd — Procedures as a Representation for Data in a Computer Program for Understanding Natural Language — 1971 — MIT AI Technical Report 235. Later descriptions of the system document users manipulating its blocks world through English commands. 20

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
SHRDLU accepted English interaction in a bounded blocks world, allowing commands to cause simulated actions such as moving and stacking objects. 21

RESEARCH OBJECT:
NATURAL-LANGUAGE OPERATIVITY predates LLMs.

LOCAL MOVE:
Winograd connects natural-language interpretation to a procedural world model and action repertoire.

SOURCE TERMS:
natural language
procedures
commands
blocks world
action

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The final paragraph says ambiguous cultural artifacts are “increasingly subjected to mechanical execution” as a historical novelty of generative models.

The relevant genealogy begins substantially earlier.

QUESTION:
What property of LLM prompting is historically new if natural-language machine control is not?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Is the novelty learned semantic breadth rather than linguistic executability?

MECHANISM:
SHRDLU:

English command
→ hand-engineered linguistic interpretation
→ procedural representation
→ blocks-world action.

LLM system:

natural-language context
→ learned model
→ distribution over continuations/actions
→ optional tool execution.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<BOUNDED HAND-SPECIFIED NL INTERPRETER>
→ <LEARNED OPEN-ENDED CONDITIONAL INTERPRETER>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Winograd’s work uses procedural representations and a deliberately restricted simulated world.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Candidate historical novelty dimensions:

    DOMAIN BREADTH
    LEARNED INTERPRETATION
    ZERO/FEW-SHOT ADAPTATION
    STOCHASTIC GENERATION
    TOOL GENERALITY

not:

    FIRST NATURAL LANGUAGE → ACTION.

TENSION:
SHRDLU’s microworld and manually engineered ontology differ radically from foundation models trained over broad corpora.

MISSING:
A genealogy from SHRDLU through:
semantic parsing,
spoken-command systems,
robotics,
instruction-following models,
LLM agents.

BOUNDARY:
LLMs can still represent a profound historical shift without being the first machine-operative natural-language interface.

CITATION TRAIL:
SHRDLU.
LUNAR.
natural-language interfaces.
semantic parsing.
robot command grounding.

TEST:
Compare SHRDLU and a tool-enabled LLM along:

vocabulary size
ontology source
parser specification
environment
action repertoire
uncertainty
adaptation.

Locate novelty empirically.

PLATFORM:
[[generative-collapse]]

===== 022 MINIMUM-025 0d355e562150389013c20b5802ff2202cfa39754545bd3f5b330ceccf7e3dba3 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
MINIMUM-025

TITLE:
The culture/computer boundary cannot rest on improvisation versus exact semantics because formally exact semantics can describe distributions of improvisatory outcomes.

SOURCE:
COLLISION — Bourdieu 1977; Dal Lago and Zorzi 2012. Bourdieu characterizes practice through regulated improvisation; probabilistic operational semantics formally assigns distributions over computational outcomes. 22

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
One source rejects social practice as conscious obedience to explicit rules; the other shows that operational semantics need not determine one unique run.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
UNDERDETERMINED BEHAVIOR and FORMAL SEMANTICS are compatible.

LOCAL MOVE:
The collision removes a false opposition between improvisation and mathematical operational description.

SOURCE TERMS:
regulated improvisation
disposition
probabilistic operational semantics
distribution
evaluation

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The conclusion treats underdetermination as if it mathematically prevents operational semantics.

Operational semantics can represent underdetermination.

QUESTION:
What kind of underdetermination cannot be captured by a formal relation or probability kernel?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Is the real cultural resistance to computation unpredictability, or the instability of the state variables and transition ontology themselves?

MECHANISM:
possible cultural formalization:

social configuration γ
→ socially conditioned relation/kernel K
→ multiple plausible practices.

The difficult part becomes:
defining γ and K faithfully.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<IMPROVISATION>
≠
<ABSENCE OF FORMAL CONSEQUENCE>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Dal Lago and Zorzi provide formal distributions of outcomes.
Bourdieu provides no formal transition machinery.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Potential social kernel:

    K : S → Δ(S)

may be formally well-defined in a model even when observed practice varies.

The empirical question is then:

    Does K preserve the mechanisms that matter?

not:

    Is K deterministic?

TENSION:
A probabilistic model can badly misrepresent social action by treating historical interpretation, reflexivity, norm contestation, and category change as fixed stochastic variables.

MISSING:
ONTOLOGICAL STABILITY.

Can S and K remain fixed while actors reinterpret the categories composing S and K?

BOUNDARY:
Probability rescues formalizability, not reductionism.

CITATION TRAIL:
practice theory.
probabilistic transition systems.
reflexive agents.
endogenous state spaces.

TEST:
Formalize one cultural practice probabilistically.

Then let actors alter the meaning of one state category itself.

If the model cannot express that without redesigning its state space, the deeper boundary lies in ONTOLOGY CHANGE rather than nondeterminism.

PLATFORM:
[[culture-computation-boundary]]

LINKS:
[[ontology-change]]
[[probability-vs-improvisation]]
[[formalization-vs-faithfulness]]

}

  year    = {2012}

===== 023 MINIMUM-026 030992304f21d4cc16e27638cced07f2ed231dcb0660fff5354830c73c6d73d7 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
MINIMUM-026

TITLE:
The deeper cultural/computational difference may be that programs usually hold their operational ontology fixed during a run while social actors can renegotiate the categories defining the state space.

SOURCE:
COLLISION — Bourdieu 1977; Deleuze and Guattari 1987; W3C SCXML 2015.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
SCXML normatively specifies in advance what counts as a state, transition, event, legal configuration, and transition-selection procedure. Bourdieu’s practices and Deleuze–Guattari’s assemblages instead make historical dispositions, authorization, and changing social classifications part of the phenomenon being explained. 23

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The strongest boundary candidate has moved from DETERMINACY to META-STABILITY.

LOCAL MOVE:
The comparison asks whether the rule system itself can change from inside the process it regulates.

SOURCE TERMS:
SCXML:
legal state configuration
transition
processor

BOURDIEU:
habitus
improvisation

DELEUZE/GUATTARI:
assemblage
incorporeal transformation

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Human interpretation is not problematic merely because multiple responses are possible.

The harder case is when participants can change:

what the states are,
what the rules mean,
who is authorized,
and what counts as valid execution.

QUESTION:
Must a programming language hold F, I, S, and T fixed during execution?

DEEPER QUESTION:
What happens when a system can rewrite its own interpreter, state ontology, or authority structure?

MECHANISM:
CLASSICAL FIXED SEMANTICS:

    <F,I,S,T> fixed
    e causes transitions within S.

REFLEXIVE SOCIAL PROCESS:

    γ_t
    → action
    → modifies not only γ
      but possibly F,I,S,T themselves.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<STATE TRANSITION>
→ <SEMANTICS / ONTOLOGY TRANSITION>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
SCXML provides a fixed normative interpretation algorithm. 24

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Ordinary:

    T_t : S → Δ(S)

Reflexive:

    <S_t,T_t,I_t>
        → 
    <S_{t+1},T_{t+1},I_{t+1}>

The semantics itself becomes state.

TENSION:
Programming systems can also support:
reflection,
self-modifying code,
dynamic linking,
metaprogramming,
language extension,
and interpreter rewriting.

Therefore even META-STABILITY may be a gradient, not an absolute boundary.

MISSING:
A theory of self-modifying semantic systems.

BOUNDARY:
This is a new research hypothesis, not a conclusion supplied by any single source.

CITATION TRAIL:
reflection.
self-interpreters.
metaobject protocols.
institutional change.
endogenous ontology.

TEST:
Construct:
A. fixed-transition program,
B. self-modifying interpreter,
C. institution whose participants can alter its rules.

Compare not output unpredictability but WHICH COMPONENTS OF THE SEMANTIC MACHINE MAY THEMSELVES CHANGE.

[[description-becomes-operation]]

===== 024 MINIMUM-028 87fc98a6cbb4aa728df73827041134e2d4ae30bb6668ac2b4cf5a2b1e3ce947f =====

ZETTEL

ID:
MINIMUM-028

TITLE:
The surviving scientific boundary is not culture versus code but specified uptake versus underspecified uptake.

SOURCE:
COLLISION — Chomsky 1959; SCXML 2015; Bourdieu 1977; Deleuze and Guattari 1987; Winograd 1971; probabilistic operational semantics 2012. 26

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Across the sources, symbolic systems become consequential through very different receivers: rewrite systems, standardized processors, social dispositions, institutions, natural-language interpreters, and probabilistic evaluators.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
OPERATIVITY admits degrees and types.

LOCAL MOVE:
The lineage no longer supports a single anvil on which “mere description” becomes “true execution.”

It supports a stack of increasingly constrained relations.

SOURCE TERMS:
derivation
processor
practice
assemblage
procedure
operational semantics

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The final sentence seeks the exact point “where a cultural description ends and an executable operation begins.”

The sources keep moving that point.

QUESTION:
What can actually be stated rigorously without reinstalling the metaphors the project set out to destroy?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Could OPERATIVE DESCRIPTION be the larger genus whose species include programs, prompts, protocols, performatives, statecharts, and norms?

MECHANISM:
representation
→ receiver
→ uptake conditions
→ licensed behavior relation
→ realization
→ observable consequence.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<DESCRIPTION>
→ <INTERPRETABLE DESCRIPTION>
→ <OPERATIONALLY SPECIFIED DESCRIPTION>
→ <EFFECTIVELY REALIZABLE DESCRIPTION>
→ <AUTHORIZED / ACTIVATED DESCRIPTION>
→ <OBSERVABLE CONSEQUENCE>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
No single source supplies this ladder.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Replace the binary:

    DESCRIPTION | PROGRAM

with discriminators:

    FORMATION
    INTERPRETATION
    EFFECTIVITY
    TRANSITION TYPE
    ENVIRONMENT
    AUTHORITY
    REALIZATION
    OBSERVATION
    META-MUTABILITY

Then ask of any symbolic object:

    WHAT EXACTLY CAN THIS REPRESENTATION MAKE HAPPEN,
    THROUGH WHAT RECEIVER,
    UNDER WHAT CONDITIONS,
    WITH WHAT DEGREE OF FORMAL SPECIFICATION?

TENSION:
The broader category risks dissolving the useful technical specificity of “programming language.”

That word should therefore remain narrow where engineering precision matters.

MISSING:
A final criterion for PROGRAM as opposed to OPERATIVE DESCRIPTION.

BOUNDARY:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
The evidence no longer supports deterministic execution as the universal boundary.

It does support the methodological demand that every claimed operative analogy identify:
receiver,
state,
transition,
authority,
and realization rather than relying on words such as “code” or “program.”

CITATION TRAIL:
program/data duality.
probabilistic semantics.
protocol semantics.
speech-act uptake.
reflection.
prompt/tool execution.

TEST:
Take every occurrence of:

CODE
PROGRAM
EXECUTE
CONTROL
RULE

in the cultural and LLM corpus.

For each require five receipts:

    WHAT IS REPRESENTED?
    WHO/WHAT RECEIVES IT?
    WHAT TRANSFORMATION IS LICENSED?
    WHAT CONDITIONS ACTIVATE IT?
    WHAT OBSERVABLE DIFFERENCE FOLLOWS?

Reject the analogy only when those receipts fail—not merely when the transition is nondeterministic.

[[description-becomes-operation]]

[[operative-description]]

[[culture-computation-boundary]]
[[prompt-language]]

===== 025 MINIMUM-029 aca094a80430ed1fc65e8a9c1c116cafe91417af0296926ac73bd5341fc28589 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
MINIMUM-029

TITLE:
Even self-reference and recursion-theorem constructions do not require Turing completeness.

SOURCE:
Neil D. Jones — “A Swiss Pocket Knife for Computability” — 2013 — §§2.5–3.3.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Jones shows that the concrete program transformations used for the examined Second Recursion Theorem constructions can be implemented in a tiny flow-chart language with assignments and list operations but no tests or loops. He explicitly notes that this tiny language is not Turing-complete and that its programs run in constant time under the stated data-structure cost assumptions.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
SELF-REFERENCE is not evidence of UNIVERSAL COMPUTATIONAL POWER.

LOCAL MOVE:
The destructive minimum loses another candidate discriminator.

Not only ordinary programming but even significant reflective constructions can survive below Turing completeness.

SOURCE TERMS:
tiny
Second Recursion Theorem
constant time
program specialization
not Turing-complete
program generation

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Features commonly taken as signatures of deep computational reflexivity can occur inside a language too weak to compute arbitrary recursive functions.

QUESTION:
Which expressive power is actually required for program self-reference?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can reflective or operative descriptions be formally weak while remaining structurally self-referential?

MECHANISM:
tiny program
→ finite data-structure manipulations
→ generate specialized/self-referential program representation

without:
loops
tests
general recursion
Turing completeness.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<SELF-REFERENCE>
↛
<TURING COMPLETENESS>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Jones’ `tiny` programs use assignments, sequencing, constants, and list operators such as `hd`, `tl`, and `cons`, with no tests or loops.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

Separate dimensions:

    COMPUTATIONAL UNIVERSALITY
    REFLECTIVE CAPABILITY
    PROGRAM GENERATION
    EXECUTABILITY.

None should be inferred from another without proof.

TENSION:
The surrounding acceptable programming system used to state the general recursion theorem is stronger than the tiny concrete transformation language used for these constructions.

MISSING:
The exact minimum expressive basis for:
quotation,
specialization,
self-reproduction,
and semantic self-modification
considered separately.

BOUNDARY:
Reflection is not a proxy for Turing completeness.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[METASTABLE-001]]
→ semantic self-modification
→ Kleene SRT implementations
→ non-Turing-complete tiny language
→ separate meta-capacity from universal computation.

TEST:
Build a capability matrix across:

finite-state machine
tiny Jones language
simply typed lambda calculus
Scheme
Maude.

Test independently for:
self-reproduction,
program-as-data,
specialization,
interpreter definition,
semantic modification,
Turing completeness.

PLATFORM:
[[destructive-minimum]]

LINKS:
[[METASTABLE-001]]
[[turing-completeness]]
[[reflection-without-universality]]
[[destructive-minimum]]

===== 026 NLC-001 2d1850e4a4ff4331ee42ea8d993e684b56d4bb012fd5a8cabbac59189184aa99 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
NLC-001

TITLE:
By 1980 researchers were not merely issuing English commands to computers; they were constructing named subroutines in English.

SOURCE:
Alan W. Biermann and Bruce W. Ballard — “Toward Natural Language Computation I” — 1980 — pp. 71–73.

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
The Natural Language Computer allows users to type English commands and watch them execute on displayed data. Correctly executed command sequences can be named and reused as subroutines. The paper explicitly discusses programming constructs including conditionals, repetition, and procedure definition.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The genealogy moves beyond SHRDLU.

Natural-language PROGRAM CONSTRUCTION, not merely natural-language command execution, predates LLMs.

LOCAL MOVE:
NLC lets a user create a new imperative verb by specifying its procedure body through English commands.

SOURCE TERMS:
Natural Language Computer
programming
subroutine
define
if
repeat
procedure
imperative

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The historical novelty claim weakens again.

By 1980 the literature already contained:

NATURAL LANGUAGE
→ PROCEDURE DEFINITION
→ REUSABLE COMPUTATION.

QUESTION:
What remains genuinely distinctive about prompt programming if both English command execution and English procedure definition predate neural language models?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Is the LLM transition fundamentally from HAND-SPECIFIED SEMANTIC COVERAGE to LEARNED OPEN-ENDED SEMANTIC GENERALIZATION?

MECHANISM:
English commands
→ scanner
→ parser
→ flow-of-control semantics
→ sentence semantics
→ matrix operations.

For procedure construction:

English command sequence
→ name sequence as new imperative
→ stored subroutine
→ later English invocation
→ procedure execution.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<NATURAL-LANGUAGE COMMAND>
→ <REUSABLE PROCEDURE>
→ [NAMING / ABSTRACTION]
→ <EXPANDED NATURAL-LANGUAGE PROGRAMMING VOCABULARY>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
The paper presents explicit modules:

SCANNER
→ PARSER
→ FLOW-OF-CONTROL SEMANTICS
↔ SENTENCE SEMANTICS
→ MATRIX COMPUTER.

It describes user-defined imperatives and programming constructs including “if,” “repeat,” and procedure definition.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

NLC already supports something like:

    Define(name, [u₁,...,uₙ])
        → Procedure(name)

and later:

    Interpret(name(args))
        → Execute(Procedure(name), args).

TENSION:
NLC sharply restricts vocabulary, semantic domain, and syntactic form; the user must work inside a matrix-oriented microworld and begin sentences with imperative verbs.

MISSING:
A comparative measure of semantic breadth and adaptation between:

NLC
SHRDLU
semantic parsers
instruction-tuned LLMs.

BOUNDARY:
LLMs are not the origin of natural-language programming.

Their novelty must be located elsewhere.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[MINIMUM-024]]
→ Winograd / SHRDLU
→ Ballard and Biermann NLC
→ natural-language subroutines and flow control
→ learned versus hand-engineered semantic coverage.

TEST:
Give NLC-like and LLM-based systems tasks requiring:

new verb definition
procedure reuse
conditional control
reference resolution
out-of-domain vocabulary
paraphrase.

Record exactly where NLC’s hand-specified language boundary appears and where the learned model continues.

PLATFORM:
[[generative-collapse]]

LINKS:
[[MINIMUM-024]]

[[NLC]]
