===== 143 FRONTIER-001 241ec20afbfecc24a62115cd4297f458b674eef916511ce27254b22b753ab66c =====

ZETTEL

ID:
FRONTIER-001

TITLE:
Later stabilization of a surprise does not travel backward and make the maker the originator of the surprise.

SOURCE:
U.S. Copyright Office — Copyright and Artificial Intelligence, Part 2: Copyrightability — 2025; United States’ 2026 summary-judgment brief in Allen v. Perlmutter.

SOURCE URL:
https://www.copyright.gov/ai/docs/us-reply-brief-and-cross-motion-for-summary-judgment.pdf

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
The Copyright Office distinguishes AI-generated expressive material from later human-authored contributions such as selection, arrangement, and modification. In the Allen litigation, the government argues that Allen did not author the underlying Midjourney visual expression while noting that directly human-authored additions could be registered if the AI-generated material were disclaimed.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
TEMPORAL FRONTIER OF AUTHORSHIP.

LOCAL MOVE:
Keep downstream human contribution strong without retroactively reallocating the origin of an unplanned expressive event.

SOURCE TERMS:
human authorship
AI-generated material
selection
arrangement
modification
disclaimer
temporal attribution

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The same feature can have two histories: an initial occurrence whose expressive form was not human-authored and a later career in which a human deliberately preserves, relates, or transforms it.

QUESTION:
Where does human authorship begin in the history of an initially unplanned generated feature?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can attribution attach to the subsequent organization built around a surprise without attaching to the surprise’s first occurrence?

MECHANISM:
At t1 system produces x unexpectedly → at t2 human recognizes x → at t3 human commits to preserve/develop x → later interventions make surrounding structure depend on x.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<RETROSPECTIVE ADOPTION> → <FORWARD-LOOKING ATTRIBUTION FRONTIER>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
U.S. copyright doctrine requires human authorship and recognizes protectable human selection, arrangement, and modification; it does not state the temporal-frontier theory proposed here.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
If Origin(x,t1)=machine-mediated/unattributed and Commit_h(x,t2), then later human contribution may begin at t2/t3 in relations and transformations governed by x. Commit_h(x,t2) does not imply Origin_h(x,t1).

TENSION:
Later human work can become extensive enough that separating underlying generated material from human-authored organization is practically difficult.

MISSING:
Case law squarely addressing a temporally layered feature whose initial generated form is retained but whose later relational role is heavily human-shaped.

BOUNDARY:
This is a research criterion and attribution model, not a statement of current U.S. copyright law.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[RETENTION-005]] → post-generation contribution → Allen/USCO → surprise can become a future constraint without retroactive origin.

TEST:
For one unexpected feature, compare the state before its appearance, the first retention decision, and every later intervention. Attribute only what becomes prospectively constrained after recognition.

PLATFORM:
[[after-surprise]]

LINKS:
[[RETENTION-005]]
[[RETENTION-005-K]]
[[AFTERSURPRISE-001]]
[[temporal-frontier]]

  author={{U.S. Copyright Office}},
  title={Copyright and Artificial Intelligence, Part 2: Copyrightability},
  institution={U.S. Copyright Office},
  year={2025}
}

@misc{AllenGov2026Brief,
  author={{United States of America}},
  title={Reply in Support of Cross-Motion for Summary Judgment, Allen v. Perlmutter},
  year={2026},

}


===== 144 INVARIANT-002 47a20d25e4a8c795c2c17daa20cc5357e6d1f65cae96bdb3924bca329ea123fe =====

ZETTEL

ID:
INVARIANT-002

TITLE:
The authored object after surprise may be an invariant across transformations rather than the surprised feature itself.

SOURCE:
COLLISION — Sol LeWitt instruction-based variation; video-game fixation cases; current generative-control field.

SOURCE URL:
https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/F2/669/852/149312/

PASSAGE:
[OUR INFERENCE]
Across instruction-based works and variable audiovisual systems, identity can persist through changing realizations. This suggests analyzing whether a maker prospectively preserves a relation across later transformations rather than asking only who originated a local feature.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
AUTHORED INVARIANT.

LOCAL MOVE:
Shift attribution from isolated generated features toward deliberately stabilized relations that survive a sequence of later changes.

SOURCE TERMS:
invariant
equivalence relation
identity
transformation
stabilization
variable work

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
“Keep the face” or “keep the tension” is not necessarily a command to preserve pixels. It defines a relation by which later states are judged the same in one respect while changing in others.

QUESTION:
Who defines the equivalence relation by which two generated states count as preserving the same expressive thing?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can authorship attach more plausibly to the human-defined invariance criterion and its successful maintenance than to the initial stochastic instance?

MECHANISM:
Unexpected feature x appears → human identifies relation q worth preserving → subsequent transformations alter other dimensions while q is repeatedly tested and stabilized.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<FEATURE OWNERSHIP> → <RELATIONAL INVARIANT ACROSS STATES>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
No source states this general authorship rule. Stern and related cases analyze recurring audiovisual elements under variable play; the invariant theory is a synthesis.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Given states y1...yn and human-defined equivalence ≈_q, stabilization seeks y1≈_q y2≈_q...≈_q yn while intentionally changing other dimensions.

TENSION:
The invariant may be supplied largely by model priors rather than human action. Persistence alone does not show that the maker caused it to persist.

MISSING:
A causal test separating model-default invariance from invariance maintained through human interventions.

BOUNDARY:
An authored invariant is a candidate analytic object, not a recognized copyright category.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[INVARIANT-001]] → cross-realization structure → [[FRONTIER-001]] → prospectively stabilized relation after surprise.

TEST:
Ablate the human preservation interventions while holding model/workflow constant. If q persists anyway, the claimed human stabilization is weak.

PLATFORM:
[[after-surprise]]

LINKS:
[[INVARIANT-001]]
[[FRONTIER-001]]
[[expressive-invariant]]
[[equivalence-relation]]

BIBTEX:
NONE


===== 145 SEARCH-001 53a84cc166cf5bbdccd98da855b996ec65344074cc0ea2b527343904cc96b758 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
SEARCH-001

TITLE:
Exploration differs from shopping when an intermediate artifact changes the operations the maker can subsequently perform.

SOURCE:
COLLISION — Kirsh and Maglio on epistemic action; Donald Schön on reflective designing; U.S. Copyright Office treatment of repeated prompting.

SOURCE URL:
https://doi.org/10.1207/s15516709cog1804_1

PASSAGE:
[OUR INFERENCE]
Browsing candidates and exploratory making can share the surface sequence generate-inspect-reject. The stronger distinction is whether an intermediate result becomes an experimental surface that changes later operative knowledge and constraints rather than merely changing which candidate is preferred.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
EXPLORATION VERSUS SHOPPING.

LOCAL MOVE:
Replace iteration count and rejection count with a test of whether observation changes the structure of subsequent interventions.

SOURCE TERMS:
exploration
selection
shopping
epistemic action
reflection-in-action
operation

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A shopper can learn what they like while browsing. Therefore preference refinement alone does not distinguish creative exploration from search through supplied alternatives.

QUESTION:
What must change after an intermediate output for a process to count as exploratory making rather than candidate shopping?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Does the artifact teach the maker a new operation, variable, or constraint that causally structures subsequent production?

MECHANISM:
candidate x appears → either (A) preference updates and x is selected/rejected, or (B) maker extracts relation/operation r → later actions are reorganized around r.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<ITERATION COUNT> → <STRUCTURAL CHANGE IN SUBSEQUENT ACTION>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Kirsh/Maglio and Schön provide the underlying epistemic and reflective mechanisms; the exploration-versus-shopping discriminator is our synthesis.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Exploratory event e_t has operational residue if it changes the set or policy of later interventions: A_{t+1} or π_{t+1} differs because of knowledge acquired from e_t.

TENSION:
Exploration can still be trivial, and shopping can involve sophisticated judgment. This distinction concerns process structure, not artistic merit.

MISSING:
A robust method for inferring operational residue from behavior without requiring the maker to narrate intentions.

BOUNDARY:
Exploration does not by itself settle copyright authorship.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[EPISTEMIC-001]] + [[DISCOVERY-002]] → generation can teach → distinguish learning-to-act from merely selecting among outputs.

TEST:
Blind analysts to final selected output. Give them the action trace and ask whether each discarded generation altered later control choices. Compare to random-resampling and shopping baselines.

PLATFORM:
[[after-surprise]]

LINKS:
[[EPISTEMIC-001]]
[[DISCOVERY-002]]
[[RETENTION-005-M-A-A]]
[[exploration-vs-shopping]]

NONE


===== 146 CRAFT-002 a2bd0d4be5e9471fa3fd7b6b52575ba6fcfaf5e1fbc58927a47441752d3b41a2 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
CRAFT-002

TITLE:
Prompt craft may chiefly produce a local causal model of the generator rather than a protectable expressive object.

SOURCE:
Minsuk Chang et al. — “The Prompt Artists” — 2023; Shachar Don-Yehiya, Leshem Choshen, and Omri Abend — “Human Learning by Model Feedback” — EMNLP 2023; 17 U.S.C. §102(b).

SOURCE URL:
https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.253/

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Studies of experienced prompting document specialized vocabularies, reusable practices, and learning from model feedback. Copyright law separately excludes procedures, processes, systems, and methods of operation as such from protection.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
LOCAL CAUSAL MODEL AS CRAFT.

LOCAL MOVE:
Separate learned ability to predict and steer a system from ownership of the resulting output or of the method itself.

SOURCE TERMS:
craft
know-how
prompt vocabulary
model feedback
method of operation
causal model

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A long failed trajectory may produce little protectable output while producing substantial technical-artistic knowledge about what the system will do under nearby interventions.

QUESTION:
Can prompt practice be highly skilled even where its central learned object is method-like and largely outside copyright?

DEEPER QUESTION:
What should count as evidence that a practitioner has learned a transferable local model rather than accumulated model-specific superstition?

MECHANISM:
repeated intervention → observed consequence → updated expectation Ĝ_h → prospective prediction → successful nearby steering.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<CRAFT AS FINISHED OBJECT> → <CRAFT AS LEARNED LOCAL CONTROL MODEL>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
The cited HCI/NLP studies document practice and adaptation; copyright statute supplies the method/expression boundary. Ĝ_h is our formalization.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Ĝ_h approximates local generator behavior: intervention → expected consequence. Craft evidence strengthens when Ĝ_h supports successful prospective predictions and transfer.

TENSION:
Highly model-specific causal knowledge may evaporate after version changes and may also encode accommodation to model bias rather than general artistic expertise.

MISSING:
Prospective transfer experiments across tasks, seeds, and model versions.

BOUNDARY:
Craft, causal control, copyrightable expression, and legal authorship remain separate predicates.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[RETENTION-005-T]] → craft can be real without copyright → [[EPISTEMIC-001]] → discarded trials can update Ĝ_h.

TEST:
Ask experts and novices to predict the effect of unseen nearby prompt/control changes before generation. Measure calibration, target accuracy, invariant preservation, and cross-version transfer.

===== 147 QUESTIONSPACE-001 2fdf43a443916fe2d389b9f691f950dd75c9c90c25004ab2588a885d691796cc =====

ZETTEL

ID:
QUESTIONSPACE-001

TITLE:
A generative system can shape not only answers but which distinctions the maker learns to ask about.

SOURCE:
Shachar Don-Yehiya, Leshem Choshen, and Omri Abend — “Human Learning by Model Feedback: The Dynamics of Iterative Prompting with Midjourney” — EMNLP 2023.

SOURCE URL:
https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.253/

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Don-Yehiya and colleagues find evidence that iterative users both add omitted details and adapt their language toward patterns the model appears to handle better. The study raises concern that model feedback can bias users’ expression.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
QUESTION-SPACE SHAPING.

LOCAL MOVE:
Extend co-adaptation from vocabulary change to a hypothesis about salience: systems may differentially reward some distinctions until those distinctions become easier to notice and ask about than others.

SOURCE TERMS:
model feedback
co-adaptation
salience
preference shaping
prompt convergence
question space

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A tool can increase local proficiency while narrowing the practical vocabulary of differences that receive useful feedback.

QUESTION:
Which distinctions stop being noticed because the system does not make them actionable or rewarding?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can a creative interface increase the user’s control over a local region while shrinking the space of distinctions the user learns to value?

MECHANISM:
model affordance/default → differential feedback quality → user attention shifts toward responsive dimensions → vocabulary/preferences evolve → future prompts further concentrate there.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<SYSTEM ANSWERS QUESTIONS> → <SYSTEM SHAPES THE SPACE OF ASKABLE QUESTIONS>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
The source establishes adaptation toward model-preferred language, not the stronger claim that the full space of artistic questions is narrowed. That extension remains a hypothesis.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Let Q_t be distinctions available to the maker as actionable questions. Feedback can alter salience weights over Q_t and may affect which new q enter Q_{t+1}.

TENSION:
All media shape attention. Medium formation is not automatically capture.

MISSING:
A task-independent baseline for user attention and preference that does not assume a pristine pre-medium intention.

BOUNDARY:
Question-space shaping is an open empirical claim, not evidence that model users lose agency.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[RETENTION-005-T-B]] → adaptation to model preferences → extend cautiously from prompt language to salience and question formation.

TEST:
Before interaction, elicit target distinctions through sketches/references/free description. After extended use, repeat with a novel model and compare which distinctions are spontaneously articulated and pursued.

PLATFORM:
[[after-surprise]]

LINKS:
[[RETENTION-005-T-B]]
[[RETENTION-005-R-A]]
[[question-space]]
[[co-adaptation]]

===== 148 CHANNEL-001 abc161c5b50169b4704a6561658cee3c0a35a65638d5204b3821bdcfcdfca220 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
CHANNEL-001

TITLE:
Creative control is a perceptible action-to-future channel, not a count of available controls.

SOURCE:
Tobias Jung, Daniel Polani, and Peter Stone — “Empowerment for Continuous Agent-Environment Systems” — Adaptive Behavior 19(1) — 2011.

SOURCE URL:
https://doi.org/10.1177/1059712310392389

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
Empowerment measures an agent’s potential influence in terms of the information-theoretic channel from actions to future observable states. Different actions that cannot produce distinguishable future observations contribute little effective control regardless of nominal option count.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
CREATIVE CHANNEL CAPACITY.

LOCAL MOVE:
Give “a button is not evidence of agency” a technical lower bound: actions matter for practical steering when they generate reliably distinguishable, observable consequences.

SOURCE TERMS:
empowerment
channel capacity
controllability
observability
action
future state

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
One text box can provide more effective artistic leverage than dozens of sliders, while many visible controls can collapse onto nearly identical futures.

QUESTION:
Can creative interfaces be evaluated by the number of artistically meaningful future differences a user can reliably produce and perceive?

DEEPER QUESTION:
How should the state representation be chosen so information-theoretic control measures meaningful artistic distinctions rather than trivial pixel changes?

MECHANISM:
action distribution A → system dynamics → future observable artifact state Y; channel capacity measures distinguishable influence available through the action channel.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<CONTROL COUNT> → <EFFECTIVE DISTINGUISHABLE FUTURES>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Jung, Polani, and Stone formalize empowerment for agent-environment systems; “creative channel capacity” is our application.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
E_creative(s)=max_{p(a)} I(Φ(Y_future);A | s), where Φ is a task/observer-relative representation of artistically meaningful differences.

TENSION:
High empowerment does not imply authorship: a game player can have strong control over trajectories inside an upstream-authored expressive system.

MISSING:
A defensible Φ for artistic meaning and a method to estimate capacity in high-dimensional stochastic generators.

BOUNDARY:
Empowerment is evidence of effective influence, not of legal authorship, artistic quality, or originality.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[AGENCY-001]] → visible controls versus leverage → [[EMPOWERMENT-001]] → action/future channel → creative application.

TEST:
Compare interfaces with similar control counts. Estimate intervention-to-feature mutual information and compare it with expert judgments of controllability and successful target editing.

PLATFORM:
[[after-surprise]]

LINKS:
[[AGENCY-001]]
[[EMPOWERMENT-001]]
[[creative-channel-capacity]]
[[effective-options]]

  author={Jung, Tobias and Polani, Daniel and Stone, Peter},
  title={Empowerment for Continuous Agent-Environment Systems},
  journal={Adaptive Behavior},
  volume={19},
  number={1},
  pages={16--39},
  year={2011},
  doi={10.1177/1059712310392389}

===== 149 ATTRIBUTION-001 5544737ca04c2b4bda281b9d93dc7fe5999ef67e09d582226b29c1cc3561c673 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
ATTRIBUTION-001

TITLE:
Control decomposes into discovery, commitment, constraint, steering, stabilization, invariant origin, and possibility-space origin.

SOURCE:
Stern Electronics, Inc. v. Kaufman — 669 F.2d 852 — Second Circuit — 1982; Midway Manufacturing Co. v. Artic International, Inc. — 704 F.2d 1009 — Seventh Circuit — 1983.

SOURCE URL:
https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/F2/704/1009/107395/

PASSAGE:
[PARAPHRASE]
The video-game cases recognize substantial player-dependent variation and control while locating protected recurring audiovisual expression upstream in the game’s creators. Player control over a trajectory therefore cannot by itself establish authorship of the possibility structure.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
FUNCTIONAL ATTRIBUTION LEDGER.

LOCAL MOVE:
Use game cases as a limiting counterexample to any theory that turns successful steering directly into authorship.

SOURCE TERMS:
player control
trajectory
possibility space
recurring expression
authorship
performance
attribution

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A person can repeatedly make the future go otherwise without being the source of the expressive grammar within which those alternatives are reachable.

QUESTION:
Which functions in a generative workflow are performed by the human, model, interface, randomness, or prior authored system?

DEEPER QUESTION:
What extra condition beyond consequential steering distinguishes authorship from performance inside an upstream-structured expressive space?

MECHANISM:
decompose production into functions: discover distinction; commit; constrain; steer; stabilize; originate invariant; originate reachable possibility structure.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<HUMAN CONTROL PERCENTAGE> → <FUNCTIONALLY TYPED ATTRIBUTION>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
The cases concern video games and fixation/copyright of audiovisual works, not generative-AI authorship. The functional ledger is our synthesis.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
For each consequential relation r record actors for Discover(r), Commit(r), Constrain(r), Steer(r), Stabilize(r), OriginInvariant(r), and OriginSpace(r). Multiple roles may differ.

TENSION:
Possibility spaces in learned generative models are not analogous to finite libraries of pre-authored game frames; model outputs are synthesized.

MISSING:
A theory of possibility-space origin suitable for learned models rather than classical game programs.

BOUNDARY:
Counterfactual control is neither trivial nor sufficient. The ledger explains agency without deciding copyright automatically.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[RETENTION-005-S-A]] → game player as poison pill for control=authorship → functional decomposition.

TEST:
Apply the ledger to one game performance, one procedural artwork, one generative image workflow, and one direct drawing. Compare where the functions diverge.

PLATFORM:
[[after-surprise]]

LINKS:
[[RETENTION-005-S-A]]
[[PLAYER-001]]
[[CHANNEL-001]]
[[functional-attribution]]

BIBTEX:
@misc{SternElectronics1982,
  title={Stern Electronics, Inc. v. Kaufman},
  howpublished={669 F.2d 852 (2d Cir. 1982)},
  year={1982}
}

@misc{MidwayArtic1983,
  title={Midway Manufacturing Co. v. Artic International, Inc.},
  howpublished={704 F.2d 1009 (7th Cir. 1983)},
  year={1983}
}


===== 150 AFTERSURPRISE-001 f8be08967067d5720b6fef1988737286710b195b0e9330e34347a916531c4077 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
AFTERSURPRISE-001

TITLE:
Authorship after surprise begins when an unforeseen difference becomes a constraint on the future.

SOURCE:
COMPILER SYNTHESIS — Schön 1992; Kirsh and Maglio 1994; U.S. Copyright Office 2025; Allen v. Perlmutter government brief 2026; Stern 1982; Midway 1983.

SOURCE URL:
https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01580516

PASSAGE:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
The synthesis proposes a temporal receipt for exploratory generative making: SURPRISE → DISCOVERY → COMMITMENT → INTERVENTION → STABILIZATION. The key evidentiary event is not the first accidental appearance but the later moment at which the discovered difference constrains what the maker subsequently allows, changes, rejects, or preserves.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
AFTER-SURPRISE RECEIPT.

LOCAL MOVE:
Narrow the field’s “call the shot” criterion so it can admit discovery without allowing retrospective intention laundering.

SOURCE TERMS:
surprise
discovery
commitment
intervention
stabilization
temporal frontier
constraint

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Prospective intention is too strict for exploratory art, while post-hoc liking is too weak. A commitment made after discovery but before later consequential transformations occupies the missing middle.

QUESTION:
What evidence shows that a surprise became a genuine constraint on future making rather than a story attached after the artifact was finished?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can behavioral stabilization after t2 provide a falsifiable receipt of human agency without reallocating authorship of the surprise at t1?

MECHANISM:
SURPRISE x at t1 → DISCOVER relation q at t2 → COMMIT q at t3 → perform interventions whose success/failure depends on q → stabilize q across later states.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<CALL THE SHOT BEFORE ALL GENERATION> → <DISCOVER, THEN MAKE THE DISCOVERY GOVERN WHAT COMES NEXT>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
No cited source states this criterion. It is a compiler synthesis designed to survive the tension between exploratory making, non-retroactive attribution, and control-not-authorship counterexamples.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Receipt R_q is strong when: (1) q first becomes perceptible at t1; (2) later action indicates q matters; (3) interventions after commitment predictably preserve/develop q; (4) alternative paths that violate q are rejected or corrected; (5) removing those interventions materially reduces q-stability.

TENSION:
A maker can perform the receipt while acting inside a possibility space whose expressive grammar comes from upstream. The receipt establishes practical agency over a subsequent relation, not automatic legal authorship.

MISSING:
Empirical thresholds for commitment and stabilization; treatment of tacit nonverbal commitments; relation to fixation and protectable expression.

BOUNDARY:
The criterion is a research wager and testable analytic proposal, not current law.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[FRONTIER-001]] + [[DISCOVERY-002]] + [[SEARCH-001]] + [[CHANNEL-001]] + [[ATTRIBUTION-001]] → temporal receipt after surprise.

TEST:
Record complete sessions. Identify surprise events without using final outcome labels. Then ask whether subsequent behavior creates prospective constraints and whether those constraints survive intervention ablation.

PLATFORM:
[[after-surprise]]

LINKS:
[[FRONTIER-001]]
[[DISCOVERY-002]]
[[SEARCH-001]]
[[CHANNEL-001]]
[[ATTRIBUTION-001]]
[[after-surprise-receipt]]

BIBTEX:
NONE


===== 151 SOURCE-COLLISION-001 196738d2cee6abdfc32de371e58bfbb3768c546b88d13f776b96ffeffa77ab78 =====

ZETTEL

ID:
SOURCE-COLLISION-001

TITLE:
Schön’s 1992 “Designing as Reflective Conversation” exists in two journal publications and should not be silently normalized.

SOURCE:
Donald A. Schön — same-title publications in Research in Engineering Design 3 (1992) and Knowledge-Based Systems 5(1) (1992).

SOURCE URL:
https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01580516

PASSAGE:
[VERIFIED BIBLIOGRAPHIC COLLISION]
Publisher and bibliographic records identify a 1992 Research in Engineering Design version (3:131–147, DOI 10.1007/BF01580516) and a 1992 Knowledge-Based Systems version (5(1):3–14, DOI 10.1016/0950-7051(92)90020-G) with the same title.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
SOURCE EDITION COLLISION.

LOCAL MOVE:
Preserve earlier cards exactly while making the edition used by the current paper explicit.

SOURCE TERMS:
Donald Schön
1992
Research in Engineering Design
Knowledge-Based Systems
DOI
bibliography conflict

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A source can be correctly remembered by title and year yet still be bibliographically ambiguous enough to corrupt page references, DOI resolution, or claims of exact verification.

QUESTION:
Are the two 1992 versions textually identical, abridged, revised, or separately typeset versions of the same essay?

DEEPER QUESTION:
What level of source identity should a research archive require before merging citations that share author, year, and title?

MECHANISM:
same author/title/year → two journal/DOI records → preserve both receipts → current paper binds its citation to the Research in Engineering Design edition actually inspected.

FORMAL SHIFT:
<SAME TITLE = SAME SOURCE> → <EDITION-SPECIFIC SOURCE IDENTITY>

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Bibliographic fact only; textual identity between editions was not exhaustively compared during this build.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION — NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
SourceIdentity should include venue/DOI/version when available, not author-title-year alone.

TENSION:
Splitting editions too aggressively can create false multiplicity when publishers legitimately reproduce the same text.

MISSING:
A page-by-page textual comparison of both complete editions.

BOUNDARY:
Do not rewrite prior immutable zettels to “correct” the venue. Add this collision card and cite the inspected edition in the current paper.

CITATION TRAIL:
[[DISCOVERY-002]] → Schön source verification → duplicate-title journal records → preserve collision rather than silently merge.

TEST:
Acquire both full texts, normalize typography only, diff paragraph order and wording, and record whether they are substantively identical.

PLATFORM:
[[after-surprise]]

LINKS:
[[DISCOVERY-002]]
[[source-identity]]
[[bibliographic-collision]]
